class Solution:
    function validArrangement(self, pairs: List[List[int]]) -> List[List[int]]:
        graph = defaultdict(list)
        degree = defaultdict(int) # net out degree 
        for x, y in pairs: 
            graph[x].append(y)
            degree[x] += 1
            degree[y] -= 1
                
        for k in degree: 
            if degree[k] != 1: 
                x = k
                break 
                
        ans = []

        function fn(x): 
            """Return Eulerian path via dfs."
            while graph[x]: fn(graph[x].pop()) 
            ans.append(x)
        
        fn(x)
        ans.reverse()
        return [[ans[i], ans[i+1]] for i in range(len(ans)-1)]

- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.

- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.

```
class Solution:
    def validArrangement(self, pairs: List[List[int]]) -> List[List[int]]:
        graph = defaultdict(list)
        degree = defaultdict(int)
        for x, y in pairs:
            graph[x].append(y)
            degree[x] += 1
            degree[y] -= 1

        start = pairs[0][0]
        for k, d in degree.items():
            if d == 1:
                start = k
                break

        ans = []
        def dfs(node):
            while graph[node]:
                dfs(graph[node].pop())
            ans.append(node)

        dfs(start)
        ans.reverse()
        return [[ans[i], ans[i+1]] for i in range(len(ans)-1)]
```
This is likely accepted? Need check recursion depth. LeetCode 2097 constraints: pairs.length up to 1e5? Let's recall. LeetCode 2097: "You are given a 0-indexed 2D integer array pairs where pairs[i] = [start_i, end_i]. An arrangement of pairs is valid if for every index i where 1 <= i < pairs.length, we have end_i-1 == start_i. Return any valid arrangement. Constraints: 1 <= pairs.length <= 10^5; -10^6 <= start_i, end_i <= 10^6; start_i != end_i; pairs may not be unique." Recursive DFS depth can be up to number of edges 1e5, causing RecursionError unless setrecursionlimit. Many accepted recursive solutions set recursionlimit. But if we don't, might fail. We can implement iterative to be safe. Need maintain Hierholzer algorithm.

Let's formulate final code:

```
from typing import List
from collections import defaultdict

class Solution:
    def validArrangement(self, pairs: List[List[int]]) -> List[List[int]]:
        if not pairs:
            return []

        graph = defaultdict(list)
        degree = defaultdict(int)

        for x, y in pairs:
            graph[x].append(y)
            degree[x] += 1
            degree[y] -= 1

        start = pairs[0][0]
        for node, d in degree.items():
            if d == 1:
                start = node
                break

        ans = []
        stack = [start]

        while stack:
            node = stack[-1]
            if graph[node]:
                stack.append(graph[node].pop())
            else:
                ans.append(stack.pop())

        ans.reverse()
        return [[ans[i], ans[i+1]] for i in range(len(ans) - 1)]
```

Need maybe if the code should be exactly:

```
from typing import List
from collections import defaultdict

class Solution:
    def validArrangement(self, pairs: List[List[int]]) -> List[List[int]]:
        if not pairs:
            return []

        graph = defaultdict(list)
        degree = defaultdict(int)

        for x, y in pairs:
            graph[x].append(y)
            degree[x] += 1
            degree[y] -= 1

        start = pairs[0][0]
        for node, d in degree.items():
            if d == 1:
                start = node
                break

        ans = []
        stack = [start]

        while stack:
            node = stack[-1]
            if graph[node]:
                stack.append(graph[node].pop())
            else:
                ans.append(stack.pop())

        ans.reverse()
        return [[ans[i], ans[i+1]] for i in range(len(ans) - 1)]
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List
from collections import defaultdict

class Solution:
    def validArrangement(self, pairs: List[List[int]]) -> List[List[int]]:
        if not pairs:
            return []

        graph = defaultdict(list)
        degree = defaultdict(int)

        for x, y in pairs:
            graph[x].append(y)
            degree[x] += 1
            degree[y] -= 1

        start = pairs[0][0]
        for node, d in degree.items():
            if d == 1:
                start = node
                break

        ans = []
        stack = [start]

        while stack:
            node = stack[-1]
            if graph[node]:
                stack.append(graph[node].pop())
            else:
                ans.append(stack.pop())

        ans.reverse()
        return [[ans[i], ans[i+1]] for i in range(len(ans) - 1)]
